### Answer: (use the provided format with backticks)

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have t chats, each with n users and m messages
2. Some messages have known senders, others have unknown senders (marked as "?")
3. Constraints:
   - No two neighboring messages can have the same sender
   - A sender cannot mention themselves in their message (mention means username appears as a complete word)
4. We need to assign senders to messages with "?" such that all constraints are satisfied
5. If impossible, output "Impossible"

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Parse the input and extract information
- Read number of chats
- For each chat: read users, messages
- Separate known and unknown messages
- Extract usernames that appear in text to identify possible mentions

### Step 2: Check if assignment is possible
- For each message with "?", determine which users can send it
- A user cannot send a message if their username appears in the text as a word
- Also, the sender cannot be the same as the previous message's sender

### Step 3: Assign senders using backtracking or greedy approach
Since constraints are moderate (n, m ≤ 100), we can use a greedy approach:
- Process messages from left to right
- For each unknown message, try possible senders that satisfy:
  1. Don't mention themselves in the text
  2. Are different from previous message's sender

### Step 4: Implementation details
- Function to check if a username appears as a word in text
- Greedy assignment: try each valid sender in order
- If no valid sender found, return impossible

```python
def find_words(text):
    """Find all words in text that are completely surrounded by non-alphanumeric or boundaries"""
    words = []
    i = 0
    n = len(text)
    
    while i < n:
        # Skip non-alphanumeric characters
        while i < n and not text[i].isalnum():
            i += 1
        
        if i >= n:
            break
            
        # Found start of word
        start = i
        while i < n and text[i].isalnum():
            i += 1
        end = i
        
        # Check if this is a complete word (not part of a larger word)
        word = text[start:end]
        
        # Add to words list
        words.append(word)
        
    return words

def can_send(sender, text, users):
    """Check if sender can send a message with given text"""
    # Check if sender appears in text as a word
    words = find_words(text)
    if sender in words:
        return False
    
    return True

def solve_chat(users, messages):
    # List of messages with their current state
    # Each message is (text, is_known, known_sender)
    result = []
    
    for i, msg in enumerate(messages):
        if msg.startswith('?'):
            result.append((None, msg[1:]))
        else:
            # Split by colon
            parts = msg.split(':', 1)
            username = parts[0]
            text = parts[1]
            result.append((username, text))
    
    # Try to assign senders to messages with unknown senders
    # Process from left to right
    assignment = [None] * len(result)
    
    for i in range(len(result)):
        if result[i][0] is not None:
            # Known sender
            assignment[i] = result[i][0]
        else:
            # Unknown sender
            # Check if it's possible to assign
            valid_senders = []
            
            for user in users:
                # Check if user can send this message (doesn't mention themselves)
                if can_send(user, result[i][1], users):
                    valid_senders.append(user)
            
            # Check previous message constraint
            if i > 0:
                prev_sender = assignment[i-1]
                if prev_sender is not None:
                    valid_senders = [user for user in valid_senders if user != prev_sender]
            
            if not valid_senders:
                return None  # Impossible
            
            # Choose first valid sender
            assignment[i] = valid_senders[0]
    
    # Verify that the solution is valid
    for i in range(len(assignment)):
        if assignment[i] is not None:
            msg_text = result[i][1]
            if not can_send(assignment[i], msg_text, users):
                return None
    
    # Create final output
    final_output = []
    for i in range(len(messages)):
        if assignment[i] is not None:
            final_output.append(f"{assignment[i]}:{result[i][1]}")
        else:
            final_output.append(f"{result[i][1]}")
    
    return final_output

def main():
    t = int(input())
    
    for _ in range(t):
        n = int(input())
        users = input().split()
        
        m = int(input())
        messages = []
        for _ in range(m):
            messages.append(input().strip())
        
        result = solve_chat(users, messages)
        
        if result is None:
            print("Impossible")
        else:
            for msg in result:
                print(msg)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

```python
def find_words(text):
    """Find all words in text that are completely surrounded by non-alphanumeric or boundaries"""
    words = []
    i = 0
    n = len(text)
    
    while i < n:
        # Skip non-alphanumeric characters
        while i < n and not text[i].isalnum():
            i += 1
        
        if i >= n:
            break
            
        # Found start of word
        start = i
        while i < n and text[i].isalnum():
            i += 1
        end = i
        
        # Add to words list
        words.append(text[start:end])
        
    return words

def can_send(sender, text, users):
    """Check if sender can send a message with given text"""
    # Check if sender appears in text as a word
    words = find_words(text)
    return sender not in words

def solve_chat(users, messages):
    # List of messages with their current state
    # Each message is (text, is_known, known_sender)
    result = []
    
    for msg in messages:
        if msg.startswith('?'):
            result.append((None, msg[1:]))
        else:
            # Split by colon
            parts = msg.split(':', 1)
            username = parts[0]
            text = parts[1]
            result.append((username, text))
    
    # Try to assign senders to messages with unknown senders
    # Process from left to right
    assignment = [None] * len(result)
    
    for i in range(len(result)):
        if result[i][0] is not None:
            # Known sender
            assignment[i] = result[i][0]
        else:
            # Unknown sender
            # Check if it's possible to assign
            valid_senders = []
            
            for user in users:
                # Check if user can send this message (doesn't mention themselves)
                if can_send(user, result[i][1], users):
                    valid_senders.append(user)
            
            # Check previous message constraint
            if i > 0:
                prev_sender = assignment[i-1]
                if prev_sender is not None:
                    valid_senders = [user for user in valid_senders if user != prev_sender]
            
            if not valid_senders:
                return None  # Impossible
            
            # Choose first valid sender
            assignment[i] = valid_senders[0]
    
    # Verify that the solution is valid
    for i in range(len(assignment)):
        if assignment[i] is not None:
            msg_text = result[i][1]
            if not can_send(assignment[i], msg_text, users):
                return None
    
    # Create final output
    final_output = []
    for i in range(len(messages)):
        if assignment[i] is not None:
            final_output.append(f"{assignment[i]}:{result[i][1]}")
        else:
            final_output.append(f"?:{result[i][1]}")
    
    return final_output

def main():
    t = int(input())
    
    for _ in range(t):
        n = int(input())
        users = input().split()
        
        m = int(input())
        messages = []

        if result is None:
            print("Impossible")
        else:
            for msg in result:
                print(msg)

if __name__ == "__main__":
```

```python
def find_words(text):
    """Find all words in text that are completely surrounded by non-alphanumeric or boundaries"""
    words = []
    i = 0
    n = len(text)
    
    while i < n:
        # Skip non-alphanumeric characters
        while i < n and not text[i].isalnum():
            i += 1
        
        if i >= n:
            break
            
        # Found start of word
        start = i
        while i < n and text[i].isalnum():
            i += 1
        end = i
        
        # Add to words list
        words.append(text[start:end])
        
    return set(words)

def can_send(sender, text, users):
    """Check if sender can send a message with given text"""
    # Check if sender appears in text as a word
    words = find_words(text)
    return sender not in words
```

```python
    """Find all words in text that are completely surrounded by non-alphanumeric or boundaries"""

            i += 1
        end = i
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Recently Vladik discovered a new entertainment — coding bots for social networks. He would like to use machine learning in his bots so now he want to prepare some learning data for them.

At first, he need to download t chats. Vladik coded a script which should have downloaded the chats, however, something went wrong. In particular, some of the messages have no information of their sender. It is known that if a person sends several messages in a row, they all are merged into a single message. It means that there could not be two or more messages in a row with the same sender. Moreover, a sender never mention himself in his messages.

Vladik wants to recover senders of all the messages so that each two neighboring messages will have different senders and no sender will mention himself in his messages.

He has no idea of how to do this, and asks you for help. Help Vladik to recover senders in each of the chats!


-----Input-----

The first line contains single integer t (1 ≤ t ≤ 10) — the number of chats. The t chats follow. Each chat is given in the following format.

The first line of each chat description contains single integer n (1 ≤ n ≤ 100) — the number of users in the chat.

The next line contains n space-separated distinct usernames. Each username consists of lowercase and uppercase English letters and digits. The usernames can't start with a digit. Two usernames are different even if they differ only with letters' case. The length of username is positive and doesn't exceed 10 characters.

The next line contains single integer m (1 ≤ m ≤ 100) — the number of messages in the chat. The next m line contain the messages in the following formats, one per line:   <username>:<text> — the format of a message with known sender. The username should appear in the list of usernames of the chat.  <?>:<text> — the format of a message with unknown sender. 

The text of a message can consist of lowercase and uppercase English letter, digits, characters '.' (dot), ',' (comma), '!' (exclamation mark), '?' (question mark) and ' ' (space). The text doesn't contain trailing spaces. The length of the text is positive and doesn't exceed 100 characters.

We say that a text mention a user if his username appears in the text as a word. In other words, the username appears in a such a position that the two characters before and after its appearance either do not exist or are not English letters or digits. For example, the text "Vasya, masha13 and Kate!" can mention users "Vasya", "masha13", "and" and "Kate", but not "masha".

It is guaranteed that in each chat no known sender mention himself in his messages and there are no two neighboring messages with the same known sender.


-----Output-----

Print the information about the t chats in the following format:

If it is not possible to recover senders, print single line "Impossible" for this chat. Otherwise print m messages in the following format:

<username>:<text>

If there are multiple answers, print any of them.


-----Examples-----

1
2
Vladik netman
2
?: Hello, Vladik!
?: Hi

Output
netman: Hello, Vladik!
Vladik: Hi

Input
1
2
netman vladik
3
netman:how are you?
?:wrong message
vladik:im fine

Output
Impossible

2
?: users are netman, vladik, Fedosik
vladik: something wrong with this chat

?: yes, netman

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have t chats, each with n users and m messages
2. Some messages have known senders, some have unknown senders (marked as "?")
3. Constraints:
   - No two neighboring messages can have the same sender
   - A sender cannot mention themselves in their message
   - If a person sends multiple messages in a row, they're merged into one message
4. We need to assign senders to messages with "?" so all constraints are satisfied

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Parse the input and extract information
- Read t chats
- For each chat, read users, messages with known/unknown senders

### Step 2: Identify constraints
- Neighboring messages must have different senders
- No sender can mention themselves in their message

### Step 3: Algorithm approach
1. First, identify all messages with known senders and their constraints
2. For messages with unknown senders ("?"), we need to assign senders satisfying:
   - Different from both previous and next message senders (if they exist)
   - The sender does not appear in the message text (as a whole word)

### Step 4: Implementation strategy
- For each "?" message, try to assign a sender from available users
- Check if the sender appears in the message text (as a word)
- Check if the sender is different from adjacent messages
- If no valid assignment exists, output "Impossible"

### Step 5: Implementation

```python
def find_words_in_text(text):
    """Find all words in text (sequences of letters and digits surrounded by non-alphanumeric)"""
    words = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(text):
        if text[i].isalnum():
            start = i
            while i < len(text) and text[i].isalnum():
                i += 1
            words.append(text[start:i])
        else:
            i += 1
    return words

def can_assign_sender(message_text, possible_senders):
    """Check if any sender in possible_senders doesn't appear in message_text as a word"""
    words = find_words_in_text(message_text)
    for sender in possible_senders:
        if sender in words:
            return False
    return True

def solve_chat(users, messages):
    n = len(messages)
    # Convert to list of tuples (sender, text) or (None, text) for unknown
    message_data = []
    for sender, text in messages:
        if sender is None:
            message_data.append((None, text))
        else:
            message_data.append((sender, text))
    
    # Check if known senders violate constraints
    # First, check if any known sender mentions themselves
    for i, (sender, text) in enumerate(message_data):
        if sender is not None:
            words = find_words_in_text(text)
            if sender in words:
                return "Impossible"
    
    # For messages with unknown senders, try to assign senders
    # We'll use a greedy approach: try to assign valid senders
    
    # Check if adjacent messages already have same sender
    for i in range(n - 1):
        if message_data[i][0] == message_data[i + 1][0] and message_data[i][0] is not None:
            return "Impossible"
    
    # Process each message with unknown sender
    for i in range(n):
        if message_data[i][0] is not None:
            continue
            
        # Find valid senders for this message
        valid_senders = []
        for user in users:
            # Check if user appears in message text as a word
            words = find_words_in_text(message_data[i][1])
            if user not in words:
                # Check if user is different from previous and next senders
                prev_sender = message_data[i-1][0] if i > 0 else None
                next_sender = message_data[i+1][0] if i < n-1 else None
                
                # If prev_sender is not None, user must not be same as prev_sender
                if prev_sender is not None and user == prev_sender:
                    continue
                    
                # If next_sender is not None, user must not be same as next_sender  
                if next_sender is not None and user == next_sender:
                    continue
                    
                valid_senders.append(user)
        
        if not valid_senders:
            return "Impossible"
            
        # Assign any valid sender
        message_data[i] = (valid_senders[0], message_data[i][1])
    
    # Verify final solution
    for i in range(n - 1):
        if message_data[i][0] == message_data[i + 1][0]:
            return "Impossible"
    
    # Check self-mention for all messages
    for sender, text in message_data:
        if sender is not None:
            words = find_words_in_text(text)
            if sender in words:
                return "Impossible"
    
    # If we get here, the assignment is valid
    result = []
    for sender, text in message_data:
        result.append(f"{sender}:{text}")
    return result

# Main execution
t = int(input())
for _ in range(t):
    n = int(input())
    users = input().split()
    m = int(input())
    
    messages = []
    for _ in range(m):
        line = input().strip()
        if line.startswith('?'):
            sender, text = None, line[1:].strip()
        else:
            sender, text = line.split(':', 1)
        messages.append((sender, text))
    
    result = solve_chat(users, messages)
    
    if result == "Impossible":
        print("Impossible")
    else:
        for msg in result:
            print(msg)
```

```python
                        result.append(f"{valid_senders[0]}:{text}")
            
            if valid:
                for msg in result:
                    print(msg)
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Recently Vladik discovered a new entertainment — coding bots for social networks. He would like to use machine learning in his bots so now he want to prepare some learning data for them.

At first, he need to download t chats. Vladik coded a script which should have downloaded the chats, however, something went wrong. In particular, some of the messages have no information of their sender. It is known that if a person sends several messages in a row, they all are merged into a single message. It means that there could not be two or more messages in a row with the same sender. Moreover, a sender never mention himself in his messages.

Vladik wants to recover senders of all the messages so that each two neighboring messages will have different senders and no sender will mention himself in his messages.

He has no idea of how to do this, and asks you for help. Help Vladik to recover senders in each of the chats!


-----Input-----

The first line contains single integer t (1 ≤ t ≤ 10) — the number of chats. The t chats follow. Each chat is given in the following format.

The first line of each chat description contains single integer n (1 ≤ n ≤ 100) — the number of users in the chat.

The next line contains n space-separated distinct usernames. Each username consists of lowercase and uppercase English letters and digits. The usernames can't start with a digit. Two usernames are different even if they differ only with letters' case. The length of username is positive and doesn't exceed 10 characters.

The next line contains single integer m (1 ≤ m ≤ 100) — the number of messages in the chat. The next m line contain the messages in the following formats, one per line:   <username>:<text> — the format of a message with known sender. The username should appear in the list of usernames of the chat.  <?>:<text> — the format of a message with unknown sender. 

The text of a message can consist of lowercase and uppercase English letter, digits, characters '.' (dot), ',' (comma), '!' (exclamation mark), '?' (question mark) and ' ' (space). The text doesn't contain trailing spaces. The length of the text is positive and doesn't exceed 100 characters.

We say that a text mention a user if his username appears in the text as a word. In other words, the username appears in a such a position that the two characters before and after its appearance either do not exist or are not English letters or digits. For example, the text "Vasya, masha13 and Kate!" can mention users "Vasya", "masha13", "and" and "Kate", but not "masha".

It is guaranteed that in each chat no known sender mention himself in his messages and there are no two neighboring messages with the same known sender.


-----Output-----

Print the information about the t chats in the following format:

If it is not possible to recover senders, print single line "Impossible" for this chat. Otherwise print m messages in the following format:

<username>:<text>

If there are multiple answers, print any of them.


-----Examples-----

1
2
Vladik netman
2
?: Hello, Vladik!
?: Hi

Output
netman: Hello, Vladik!
Vladik: Hi

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have t chats, each with n users and m messages
2. Some messages have known senders (username:text), others have unknown senders (?:text)
3. Rules:
   - No two consecutive messages can have the same sender
   - A sender cannot mention themselves in their message (mention means username appears as a whole word)
   - We need to assign senders to all unknown messages

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Parse Input and Extract Information
- Read number of chats
- For each chat: read users, messages
- For each message, determine if sender is known or unknown

### Step 2: Check Constraints
- For each message with unknown sender, check which users are valid (don't mention themselves)
- For each pair of consecutive messages, ensure senders are different

### Step 3: Solve the Assignment
- Use backtracking or greedy approach to assign valid senders
- Start from first unknown message and try possible assignments
- Check constraints after each assignment

### Step 4: Implementation Strategy
1. For each message with unknown sender, determine valid senders (those not mentioned in their text)
2. For consecutive messages, ensure senders are different
3. Use constraint satisfaction to find a valid assignment

## Code Implementation

```python
def get_mentions(text, usernames):
    """Extract all words from text that are in usernames"""
    mentions = set()
    words = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(text):
        if text[i].isalnum():
            word_start = i
            while i < len(text) and text[i].isalnum():
                i += 1
            word = text[word_start:i]
            words.append(word)
        else:
            i += 1
    
    # Check which of these words are in usernames
    for word in words:
        if word in usernames:
            mentions.add(word)
    return mentions

def solve_chat(users, messages):
    # Extract known senders and unknown messages
    known_senders = []
    unknown_indices = []
    
    for i, msg in enumerate(messages):
        if msg.startswith('?'):
            unknown_indices.append(i)
        else:
            # Parse known sender
            parts = msg.split(':', 1)
            sender = parts[0]
            text = parts[1]
            known_senders.append((i, sender, text))
    
    # Check if any known sender mentions themselves
    for i, sender, text in known_senders:
        if sender in get_mentions(text, users):
            return "Impossible"
    
    # If there are no unknown messages, we're done
    if not unknown_indices:
        return messages
    
    # For each unknown message, find valid senders (not mentioned in text)
    valid_senders = []
    for idx in unknown_indices:
        text = messages[idx].split(':', 1)[1]
        mentions = get_mentions(text, users)
        valid_senders.append([user for user in users if user not in mentions])
    
    # Now we need to assign senders to unknown messages
    # We'll use backtracking to find a valid assignment
    def backtrack(idx, assignments):
        if idx == len(unknown_indices):
            # Check consecutive message constraint
            result = messages.copy()
            for i, (orig_idx, _, _) in enumerate(known_senders):
                if orig_idx == idx:
                    continue
                # We need to handle this properly - let's reconstruct differently
            return result
        
        # Try each valid sender for current unknown message
        for sender in valid_senders[idx]:
            # Check if this sender conflicts with previous message
            prev_idx = unknown_indices[idx - 1] if idx > 0 else -1
            if idx > 0:
                # Previous message in the unknown list
                prev_sender = assignments[idx - 1]
                if sender == prev_sender:
                    continue
            
            # Check if this sender conflicts with next message
            next_idx = unknown_indices[idx + 1] if idx + 1 < len(unknown_indices) else None
            if next_idx is not None:
                # We'll check this after assignment
                pass
            
            # Check if sender is different from immediately preceding message
            prev_known_idx = -1
            for i, (known_idx, _, _) in enumerate(known_senders):
                if known_idx < unknown_indices[idx] and known_idx > unknown_indices[idx] - 1:
                    prev_known_idx = known_idx
                    break
            
            # Check that sender doesn't equal the sender of the immediately preceding (known or unknown) message
            # Find the message that comes immediately before this one
            prev_msg_idx = -1
            for i in range(len(messages)):
                if i < unknown_indices[idx] and i > unknown_indices[idx] - 2:
                    prev_msg_idx = i
                    break
            
            # Instead, let's build the complete assignment and validate it at the end
            assignments[idx] = sender
            result = backtrack(idx + 1, assignments)
            if result is not None:
                return result
        
        return None
    
    # Try to find a valid assignment
    assignments = [None] * len(unknown_indices)
    result = backtrack(0, assignments)
    
    if result is None:
        return "Impossible"
    
    # Build final messages
    final_messages = messages.copy()
    for i, (orig_idx, _, _) in enumerate(known_senders):
        # This message already has a sender
        pass
    
    # Actually, we need to rebuild the messages with assigned senders
    final_messages = []
    for i, msg in enumerate(messages):
        if msg.startswith('?'):
            # Replace with assigned sender
            parts = msg.split(':', 1)
            text = parts[1]
            sender = assignments[unknown_indices.index(i)]
            final_messages.append(f"{sender}:{text}")
        else:
            final_messages.append(msg)
    
    # Now validate the final solution
    # Check consecutive messages have different senders
    for i in range(len(final_messages) - 1):
        msg1 = final_messages[i]
        msg2 = final_messages[i + 1]
        sender1 = msg1.split(':', 1)[0]
        sender2 = msg2.split(':', 1)[0]
        if sender1 == sender2:
            return "Impossible"
    
    # Check each sender doesn't mention themselves
    for i, msg in enumerate(final_messages):
        if msg.startswith('?'):
            continue
        sender = msg.split(':', 1)[0]
        text = msg.split(':', 1)[1]
        if sender in get_mentions(text, users):
            return "Impossible"
    
    return final_messages

def main():
    t = int(input())
    
    for _ in range(t):
        n = int(input())
        users = input().split()
        m = int(input())
        messages = []
        
        for _ in range(m):
            messages.append(input().strip())
        
        result = solve_chat(users, messages)
        
        if result == "Impossible":
            print("Impossible")
        else:
            for msg in result:
                print(msg)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

```python
def can_assign(messages, users):
    # First, check if any known sender mentions themselves
    for msg in messages:
        if ':' in msg:
            parts = msg.split(':', 1)
            sender = parts[0]
            text = parts[1]
            if sender in get_mentions(text, users):
                return False
    
    # Find all unknown messages and their valid senders
    unknown_indices = []
    for i, msg in enumerate(messages):
        if msg.startswith('?'):
            unknown_indices.append(i)
    
    # If no unknown messages, return the messages
    if not unknown_indices:
        return messages
    
    # For each unknown message, find valid senders (not mentioned in text)
    valid_senders = []
    for idx in unknown_indices:
        text = messages[idx].split(':', 1)[1]
        mentions = get_mentions(text, users)
        valid_senders.append([user for user in users if user not in mentions])
    
    # Try to assign senders using backtracking
    def backtrack(i, assignment):
        if i == len(unknown_indices):
            # Check consecutive messages have different senders
            assigned = []
            for j, msg in enumerate(messages):
                if msg.startswith('?'):
                    assigned.append(assignment[unknown_indices.index(j)])
                else:
                    sender = msg.split(':', 1)[0]
                    assigned.append(sender)
            
            # Check consecutive message constraints
            for j in range(len(assigned) - 1):
                if assigned[j] == assigned[j + 1]:
                    return False
            
            return True
        
        # Try each valid sender for current unknown message
        current_idx = unknown_indices[i]
        for sender in valid_senders[i]:
            # Check if this sender conflicts with previous message
            prev_idx = current_idx - 1
            if prev_idx >= 0:
                # Find the message that comes before this one
                prev_message_idx = -1
                for k in range(len(messages)):
                    if k < current_idx and k > prev_idx:
                        prev_message_idx = k
                        break
                
                # The sender of the message immediately before current_idx
                prev_sender = None
                for k in range(len(messages)):
                    if k < current_idx and k > prev_idx:
                        prev_sender = messages[k].split(':', 1)[0]
                        break
                
                # If this is a known message, its sender is already set
                # If it's unknown, we're trying to assign it
                
                # Actually, let's check the sender of the message immediately before
                # We need to find the message that comes just before current_idx
                if prev_idx >= 0:
                    prev_msg_idx = -1
                    for k in range(len(messages)):
                        if k == current_idx - 1:
                            if messages[k].startswith('?'):
                                # This is an unknown message that we haven't assigned yet
                                # This is already handled by our assignment
                                pass
                            else:
                                prev_sender = messages[k].split(':', 1)[0]
                                break
                else:
                    prev_sender = None
            
            # Check if this sender could be used
            # The constraint is that consecutive messages must have different senders
            # So we need to check against the immediately preceding message
            
            # Find the message immediately preceding current_idx
            prev_msg_idx = current_idx - 1
            if prev_msg_idx >= 0:
                prev_sender = None
                for k in range(len(messages)):
                    if k == prev_msg_idx:
                        if messages[k].startswith('?'):
                            # This is an unknown message that has a valid assignment
                            # The assignment is stored in assignment array
                            # We're at index i, so we're assigning to unknown_indices[i]
                            # We need to find which unknown this corresponds to
                            pass
                        else:
                            prev_sender = messages[k].split(':', 1)[0]
            
            # Actually, simpler approach: after assignment, just validate
            # Let's try the assignment and validate at the end
            assignment[i] = sender
            if backtrack(i + 1, assignment):
                return True
        
        return False
    
    # Try to find an assignment
    assignment = [None] * len(unknown_indices)
    if backtrack(0, assignment):
        # Build final messages
        final_messages = []
        for msg in messages:
            if msg.startswith('?'):
                idx = unknown_indices.index(msg)
                sender = assignment[idx]
                text = msg.split(':', 1)[1]
                final_messages.append(f"{sender}:{text}")
            else:
                final_messages.append(msg)
        
        # Validate consecutive messages have different senders
        for i in range(len(final_messages) - 1):
            sender1 = final_messages[i].split(':', 1)[0]
            sender2 = final_messages[i + 1].split(':', 1)[0]
            if sender1 == sender2:
                return "Impossible"
        
        # Validate no sender mentions themselves
        for msg in final_messages:
            if msg.startswith('?'):
                continue
            sender = msg.split(':', 1)[0]
            text = msg.split(':', 1)[1]
            if sender in get_mentions(text, users):
                return "Impossible"
        
        return final_messages
    
    return "Impossible"
```

```python
def get_mentions(text, usernames):
    """Extract all words from text that are in usernames"""
    mentions = set()
    words = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(text):
        if text[i].isalnum():
            word_start = i
            while i < len(text) and text[i].isalnum():
                i += 1
            word = text[word_start:i]
            words.append(word)
        else:
            i += 1
    
    # Check which of these words are in usernames
    for word in words:
        if word in usernames:
            mentions.add(word)
    return mentions

def solve_chat(messages, users):
    # First, check if any known sender mentions themselves
    for msg in messages:
        if ':' in msg:
            parts = msg.split(':', 1)
            sender = parts[0]
            text = parts[1]
            if sender in get_mentions(text, users):
                return "Impossible"
    
    # Find unknown messages
    unknown_indices = []
    for i, msg in enumerate(messages):
        if msg.startswith('?'):
            unknown_indices.append(i)
    
    # If no unknown messages, return as is
    if not unknown_indices:
        return messages
    
    # For each unknown message, determine valid senders
    valid_senders = []
    for idx in unknown_indices:
        text = messages[idx].split(':', 1)[1]
        mentions = get_mentions(text, users)
        valid_senders.append([user for user in users if user not in mentions])
    
    # Try to assign senders using backtracking
    def backtrack(idx, assignment):
        if idx == len(unknown_indices):
            # Check if consecutive messages have different senders
            senders = []
            for i, msg in enumerate(messages):
                if msg.startswith('?'):
                    pos = unknown_indices.index(i)
                    senders.append(assignment[pos])
                else:
                    senders.append(msg.split(':', 1)[0])
            
            # Check consecutive messages have different senders
            for i in range(len(senders) - 1):
                if senders[i] == senders[i + 1]:
                    return False
            
            return True
        
        # Try each valid sender for current unknown message
        current_idx = unknown_indices[idx]
        for sender in valid_senders[idx]:
            assignment[idx] = sender
            if backtrack(idx + 1, assignment):
                return True
        
        return False
    
    # Try to find a valid assignment
    assignment = [None] * len(unknown_indices)
    if not backtrack(0, assignment):
        return "Impossible"
    
    # Build final messages
    result = []
    for msg in messages:
        if msg.startswith('?'):
            idx = unknown_indices.index(msg)
            sender = assignment[idx]
            text = msg.split(':', 1)[1]
            result.append(f"{sender}:{text}")
        else:
            result.append(msg)
    
    # Final validation: check consecutive messages and self-mentions
    for i in range(len(result) - 1):
        sender1 = result[i].split(':', 1)[0]
        sender2 = result[i + 1].split(':', 1)[0]
        if sender1 == sender2:
            return "Impossible"
    
    # Check self-mention
    for msg in result:
        if ':' in msg:
            sender = msg.split(':', 1)[0]
            text = msg.split(':', 1)[1]
            if sender in get_mentions(text, users):
                return "Impossible"
    
    return result

def main():
    t = int(input())
    
    for _ in range(t):
        n = int(input())

        m = int(input())
```

The text of a message can consist of lowercase and uppercase English letter, digits, characters '.' (dot), ',' (comma), '!' (exclamation mark), '?' (question mark) and ' ' (space). The text doesn't contain trailing spaces. The length of the text is positive and doesn't exceed 100 characters.

We say that a text mention a user if his username appears in the text as a word. In other words, the username appears in a such a position that the two characters before and after its appearance either do not exist or are not English letters or digits. For example, the text "Vasya, masha13 and Kate!" can mention users "Vasya", "masha13", "and" and "Kate", but not "masha".

It is guaranteed that in each chat no known sender mention himself in his messages and there are no two neighboring messages with the same known sender.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have t chats, each with n users and m messages
2. Some messages have known senders, others have unknown senders (marked with "?")
3. Constraints:
   - No two neighboring messages can have the same sender
   - A sender cannot mention themselves in their message
   - If a person sends multiple messages in a row, they're merged into one message (so no consecutive same senders)

## Key Observations

1. We need to assign senders to messages with "?" such that:
   - No two adjacent messages have the same sender
   - No sender mentions themselves in their message

2. For messages with known senders, we need to check:
   - The sender doesn't mention themselves in their message
   - The sender is different from adjacent messages

3. For messages with "?" (unknown), we need to assign senders that satisfy:
   - Are different from adjacent messages (left and right)
   - Don't mention themselves in their message

## Algorithm

1. Parse input and extract known/unknown messages
2. For each message with known sender, verify it doesn't mention itself (given as guaranteed, but need to check)
3. For messages with "?", we need to assign senders
4. Use backtracking or greedy approach to assign senders
5. Since constraints are small (n, m ≤ 100), we can use a simple greedy approach or try reasonable assignments

## Step-by-step Solution

```python
def gets_mentions(text, usernames):
    """Find all usernames mentioned in the text"""
    mentions = set()
    words = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(text):
        if text[i].isalnum():
            word_start = i
            while i < len(text) and text[i].isalnum():
                i += 1
            word = text[word_start:i]
            words.append(word)
        else:
            i += 1
    
    for word in words:
        if word in usernames:
            mentions.add(word)
    
    return mentions

def can_assign_senders(messages, usernames):
    """Try to assign senders to messages with ?"""
    n = len(messages)
    # Create a copy with current senders
    senders = [None] * n
    
    # First, fill known senders
    for i in range(n):
        if messages[i][0] != '?':
            senders[i] = messages[i][0]
    
    # Check if known senders violate constraints
    for i in range(n):
        if senders[i] is not None:
            # Check if sender mentions themselves
            text = messages[i][1]
            if senders[i] in gets_mentions(text, usernames):
                return False
            
            # Check adjacent messages
            if i > 0 and senders[i] == senders[i-1]:
                return False
            if i < n-1 and senders[i] == senders[i+1]:
                return False
    
    # Now try to assign senders to messages with ?
    # Use greedy approach: for each "?", try to assign a sender that doesn't violate constraints
    
    # Create a list of indices where sender is unknown
    unknown_indices = []
    for i in range(n):
        if messages[i][0] == '?':
            unknown_indices.append(i)
    
    # For each unknown message, try to assign a sender
    for idx in unknown_indices:
        assigned = False
        # Try each possible sender
        for sender in usernames:
            # Check if this sender would violate constraints with neighbors
            valid = True
            
            # Check left neighbor (if exists)
            if idx > 0 and senders[idx-1] == sender:
                valid = False
            
            # Check right neighbor (if exists)
            if idx < n-1 and senders[idx+1] == sender:
                valid = False
            
            # Check if sender mentions themselves in message
            if valid:
                text = messages[idx][1]
                if sender in gets_mentions(text, usernames):
                    valid = False
            
            if valid:
                senders[idx] = sender
                assigned = True
                break
        
        if not assigned:
            return False
    
    # After all assignments, verify all constraints
    for i in range(n):
        if senders[i] is not None:
            text = messages[i][1]
            # Check if sender mentions themselves
            if senders[i] in gets_mentions(text, usernames):
                return False
            
            # Check adjacent constraints
            if i > 0 and senders[i] == senders[i-1]:
                return False
            if i < n-1 and senders[i] == senders[i+1]:
                return False
    
    return True

def solve_chat(n, usernames, messages):
    # First, check if known senders already violate constraints
    for i in range(len(messages)):
        if messages[i][0] != '?':
            text = messages[i][1]
            # Check if sender mentions themselves
            if messages[i][0] in gets_mentions(text, usernames):
                return "Impossible"
    
    # Try to assign all unknown senders
    if can_assign_senders(messages, usernames):

                pass
        return result
    else:
        return "Impossible"

def solve():
    t = int(input())
    
    for _ in range(t):
        n = int(input())
        usernames = input().split()
        
        m = int(input())
        messages = []
        for _ in range(m):
            line = input().strip()
            parts = line.split(':', 1)
            sender = parts[0]
            text = parts[1]
            messages.append((sender, text))
        
        # Try to assign all senders
        found = False
        result = ["Impossible"]
        
        # For each unknown message, try to assign a valid sender
        # We'll use a more systematic approach
        
        # First, check known senders
        valid = True
        for i in range(m):
            if messages[i][0] != '?':
                text = messages[i][1]
                if messages[i][0] in gets_mentions(text, usernames):
                    valid = False
                    break
        
        if not valid:
            print("Impossible")
            continue
        
        # Check adjacent constraints for known senders
        for i in range(m):
            if messages[i][0] != '?':
                if i > 0 and messages[i][0] == messages[i-1][0]:
                    valid = False
                    break
                if i < m-1 and messages[i][0] == messages[i+1][0]:
                    valid = False
                    break
        
        if not valid:
            print("Impossible")
            continue
        
        # Now assign unknown senders
        # Create a list of indices with unknown senders
        unknown_indices = [i for i in range(m) if messages[i][0] == '?']
        # We'll try to assign senders greedily
        senders = [None] * m
        
        # Fill known senders
        for i in range(m):
            if messages[i][0] != '?':
                senders[i] = messages[i][0]
        
        # For each unknown message, assign a valid sender
        for idx in unknown_indices:
            assigned = False
            for sender in usernames:
                # Check constraints with neighbors
                left_ok = (idx == 0) or (senders[idx-1] != sender)
                right_ok = (idx == m-1) or (senders[idx+1] != sender)
                
                # Check if sender mentions themselves in message
                text = messages[idx][1]
                if sender in gets_mentions(text, usernames):
                    continue
                
                if left_ok and right_ok:
                    senders[idx] = sender
                    assigned = True
                    break
            
            if not assigned:
                print("Impossible")
                found = True
                break
        
        if found:
            continue
            
        # Verify final solution
        valid_final = True
        for i in range(m):
            if senders[i] is None:
                continue
            text = messages[i][1]
            # Check self-mention
            if senders[i] in gets_mentions(text, usernames):
                valid_final = False
                break
            # Check adjacent constraints
            if i > 0 and senders[i] == senders[i-1]:
                valid_final = False
                break
            if i < m-1 and senders[i] == senders[i+1]:
                valid_final = False
                break
                
        if valid_final:
            result = []
            for i in range(m):
                if senders[i] is not None:
                    result.append(f"{senders[i]}:{messages[i][1]}")
                else:
                    result.append(f"{messages[i][0]}:{messages[i][1]}")  # this shouldn't happen
            for msg in result:
                print(msg)
        else:
            print("Impossible")
```

```python
def gets_mentions(text, usernames):
    """Find all usernames mentioned in the text"""
    mentions = set()
    # Split text into words by non-alphanumeric characters
    words = []

            i += 1

        if word in usernames:
            mentions.add(word)
    
    return mentions
```

    """Find all usernames mentioned in the text"""
    mentions = set()

            mentions.add(word)
    
    return mentions

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Recently Vladik discovered a new entertainment — coding bots for social networks. He would like to use machine learning in his bots so now he want to prepare some learning data for them.

At first, he need to download t chats. Vladik coded a script which should have downloaded the chats, however, something went wrong. In particular, some of the messages have no information of their sender. It is known that if a person sends several messages in a row, they all are merged into a single message. It means that there could not be two or more messages in a row with the same sender. Moreover, a sender never mention himself in his messages.

Vladik wants to recover senders of all the messages so that each two neighboring messages will have different senders and no sender will mention himself in his messages.

He has no idea of how to do this, and asks you for help. Help Vladik to recover senders in each of the chats!


-----Input-----

The first line contains single integer t (1 ≤ t ≤ 10) — the number of chats. The t chats follow. Each chat is given in the following format.

The first line of each chat description contains single integer n (1 ≤ n ≤ 100) — the number of users in the chat.

The next line contains n space-separated distinct usernames. Each username consists of lowercase and uppercase English letters and digits. The usernames can't start with a digit. Two usernames are different even if they differ only with letters' case. The length of username is positive and doesn't exceed 10 characters.

The next line contains single integer m (1 ≤ m ≤ 100) — the number of messages in the chat. The next m line contain the messages in the following formats, one per line:   <username>:<text> — the format of a message with known sender. The username should appear in the list of usernames of the chat.  <?>:<text> — the format of a message with unknown sender. 

The text of a message can consist of lowercase and uppercase English letter, digits, characters '.' (dot), ',' (comma), '!' (exclamation mark), '?' (question mark) and ' ' (space). The text doesn't contain trailing spaces. The length of the text is positive and doesn't exceed 100 characters.

We say that a text mention a user if his username appears in the text as a word. In other words, the username appears in a such a position that the two characters before and after its appearance either do not exist or are not English letters or digits. For example, the text "Vasya, masha13 and Kate!" can mention users "Vasya", "masha13", "and" and "Kate", but not "masha".

It is guaranteed that in each chat no known sender mention himself in his messages and there are no two neighboring messages with the same known sender.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have t chats, each with n users and m messages
2. Some messages have unknown senders (marked as "?")
3. Constraints:
   - No two consecutive messages can have the same sender
   - A sender cannot mention themselves in their message
   - If a user mentions another user in their message, that user cannot be the sender
4. We need to assign senders to unknown messages or output "Impossible"

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Parse the input and understand the constraints

For each chat:
- Get the list of valid usernames
- Get the messages with known senders and unknown senders
- For each unknown message, we need to find a valid sender that:
  1. Is different from the previous message's sender
  2. Does not mention themselves in the message text

### Step 2: Algorithm approach

For each unknown message, we need to:
1. Check which senders are possible (not the previous sender)
2. Check if any of those senders appear in the message text (if they do, that sender is invalid)
3. If no valid sender exists, return "Impossible"
4. If multiple valid senders exist, pick any

### Step 3: Implementation details

1. Extract all usernames from the chat
2. For each message, determine if it has a known sender or is unknown
3. For unknown messages, try each valid username and check constraints
4. Use backtracking or greedy approach - since constraints are local, we can process messages sequentially

### Step 4: Handle edge cases

- When we have only one message, any sender can work (as long as it doesn't mention itself)
- When we have multiple unknown messages, we need to ensure consecutive messages have different senders

Let me implement this:

```python
def find_mentions(text, usernames):
    """Find all usernames mentioned in the text"""
    mentions = set()
    words = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(text):
        # Find word boundaries
        if text[i].isalnum():
            word_start = i
            while i < len(text) and text[i].isalnum():
                i += 1
            word = text[word_start:i]
            words.append(word)
        else:
            i += 1
    
    # Check if any word matches a username
    for word in words:
        if word in usernames:
            mentions.add(word)
    
    return mentions

def is_valid_sender(sender, text, all_usernames):
    """Check if sender can send this message (doesn't mention themselves)"""
    mentions = find_mentions(text, all_usernames)
    return sender not in mentions

def solve_chat(usernames, messages):
    n = len(usernames)
    m = len(messages)
    
    # Convert messages to list of (sender, text)
    message_list = []
    for msg in messages:
        parts = msg.split(':', 1)
        sender = parts[0]
        text = parts[1]
        message_list.append((sender, text))
    
    # First, identify which messages have known senders
    known_senders = []
    unknown_positions = []
    
    for i, (sender, text) in enumerate(message_list):
        if sender == "?":
            unknown_positions.append(i)
        else:
            known_senders.append((i, sender, text))
    
    # Process messages in order
    result = []
    prev_sender = None
    
    for i, (sender, text) in enumerate(message_list):
        if sender == "?":
            # Find valid sender for this unknown message
            valid_senders = []
            for username in usernames:
                # Cannot be same as previous sender
                if prev_sender is not None and username == prev_sender:
                    continue
                # Cannot mention themselves
                if not is_valid_sender(username, text, usernames):
                    continue
                valid_senders.append(username)
            
            if not valid_senders:
                return "Impossible"
            
            # Choose any valid sender
            chosen_sender = valid_senders[0]
            result.append((chosen_sender, text))
            prev_sender = chosen_sender
        else:
            # Known sender - just add it
            result.append((sender, text))
            prev_sender = sender
    
    return result

def main():
    t = int(input())
    
    for _ in range(t):
        n = int(input())
        usernames = input().split()
        m = int(input())
        
        messages = []
        for _ in range(m):
            messages.append(input())
        
        result = solve_chat(usernames, messages)
        
        if result == "Impossible":
            print("Impossible")
        else:
            for sender, text in result:
                print(f"{sender}:{text}")

main()
```

Wait, I need to be more careful about the algorithm. Let me reconsider.

The issue is that when we have a message with a known sender, we need to ensure that no neighboring messages have the same sender, and that the known sender doesn't mention themselves.

But in the second example:
- netman:how are you?
- ?:wrong message  
- vladik:im fine

For the middle message "?", we need to assign a sender that:
1. Is not netman (since previous message is netman)
2. Is not vladik (since next message is vladik)
3. Does not mention itself

But there are only two users: netman and vladik. So we have no choice - the middle message must be sent by either netman or vladik, but both are invalid due to the constraints.

Let me refine the solution:

```python
def find_mentions(text, usernames):
    """Find all usernames mentioned in the text"""
    mentions = set()
    words = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(text):
        # Find word boundaries - alphanumeric sequences
        if text[i].isalnum():
            word_start = i
            while i < len(text) and text[i].isalnum():
                i += 1
            word = text[word_start:i]
            words.append(word)
        else:
            i += 1
    
    # Check if any word matches a username
    for word in words:
        if word in usernames:
            mentions.add(word)
    
    return mentions

def is_valid_sender(sender, text, all_usernames):
    """Check if sender can send this message (doesn't mention themselves)"""
    mentions = find_mentions(text, all_usernames)
    return sender not in mentions

def solve_chat(usernames, messages):
    m = len(messages)
    
    # Convert messages to list of tuples
    message_list = []
    for msg in messages:
        parts = msg.split(':', 1)
        sender = parts[0]
        text = parts[1]
        message_list.append((sender, text))
    
    # Track the sender of each message
    result = []
    prev_sender = None
    
    for i, (sender, text) in enumerate(message_list):
        if sender == "?":
            # Find valid sender for this unknown message
            valid_senders = []
            for username in usernames:
                # Cannot be same as previous sender
                if prev_sender is not None and username == prev_sender:
                    continue
                # Cannot mention themselves
                if not is_valid_sender(username, text, usernames):
                    continue
                valid_senders.append(username)
            
            if not valid_senders:
                return "Impossible"
            
            # Choose any valid sender
            chosen_sender = valid_senders[0]
            result.append((chosen_sender, text))
            prev_sender = chosen_sender
        else:
            # Known sender - just add it
            result.append((sender, text))
            prev_sender = sender
    
    return result

def main():
    t = int(input())
    
    for _ in range(t):
        n = int(input())
        usernames = input().split()
        m = int(input())
        
        messages = []
        for _ in range(m):
            messages.append(input())
        
        result = solve_chat(usernames, messages)
        
        if result == "Impossible":
            print("Impossible")
        else:
            for sender, text in result:
                print(f"{sender}:{text}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Recently Vladik discovered a new entertainment — coding bots for social networks. He would like to use machine learning in his bots so now he want to prepare some learning data for them.

At first, he need to download t chats. Vladik coded a script which should have downloaded the chats, however, something went wrong. In particular, some of the messages have no information of their sender. It is known that if a person sends several messages in a row, they all are merged into a single message. It means that there could not be two or more messages in a row with the same sender. Moreover, a sender never mention himself in his messages.

Vladik wants to recover senders of all the messages so that each two neighboring messages will have different senders and no sender will mention himself in his messages.

He has no idea of how to do this, and asks you for help. Help Vladik to recover senders in each of the chats!


-----Input-----

The first line contains single integer t (1 ≤ t ≤ 10) — the number of chats. The t chats follow. Each chat is given in the following format.

The first line of each chat description contains single integer n (1 ≤ n ≤ 100) — the number of users in the chat.

The next line contains n space-separated distinct usernames. Each username consists of lowercase and uppercase English letters and digits. The usernames can't start with a digit. Two usernames are different even if they differ only with letters' case. The length of username is positive and doesn't exceed 10 characters.

The next line contains single integer m (1 ≤ m ≤ 100) — the number of messages in the chat. The next m line contain the messages in the following formats, one per line:   <username>:<text> — the format of a message with known sender. The username should appear in the list of usernames of the chat.  <?>:<text> — the format of a message with unknown sender. 

The text of a message can consist of lowercase and uppercase English letter, digits, characters '.' (dot), ',' (comma), '!' (exclamation mark), '?' (question mark) and ' ' (space). The text doesn't contain trailing spaces. The length of the text is positive and doesn't exceed 100 characters.

We say that a text mention a user if his username appears in the text as a word. In other words, the username appears in a such a position that the two characters before and after its appearance either do not exist or are not English letters or digits. For example, the text "Vasya, masha13 and Kate!" can mention users "Vasya", "masha13", "and" and "Kate", but not "masha".

It is guaranteed that in each chat no known sender mention himself in his messages and there are no two neighboring messages with the same known sender.


-----Output-----

Print the information about the t chats in the following format:

If it is not possible to recover senders, print single line "Impossible" for this chat. Otherwise print m messages in the following format:

<username>:<text>

If there are multiple answers, print any of them.


-----Examples-----
Input
1
2
Vladik netman
2
?: Hello, Vladik!
?: Hi

Output
netman: Hello, Vladik!
Vladik: Hi

Input
1
2
netman vladik
3
netman:how are you?
?:wrong message
vladik:im fine

Output
Impossible

2
3
netman vladik Fedosik
2
?: users are netman, vladik, Fedosik
vladik: something wrong with this chat
4
netman tigerrrrr banany2001 klinchuh
4
?: tigerrrrr, banany2001, klinchuh, my favourite team ever, are you ready?
klinchuh: yes, coach!
?: yes, netman
banany2001: yes of course.

Output

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have t chats, each with n users and m messages
2. Some messages have known senders (with username: text format), others have unknown senders (with ? : text format)
3. Constraints:
   - No two consecutive messages can have the same sender
   - A sender cannot mention themselves in their message (the username must not appear as a word in the text)
4. We need to assign senders to all messages (including unknown ones) or output "Impossible" if it's not possible

## Solution Approach

1. For each chat, we need to:
   - Parse the known senders and their messages
   - For unknown messages, try to assign senders that satisfy the constraints
   - Check if the assignment is valid (no consecutive same senders, no self-mention)

2. Strategy:
   - First, identify which messages have known senders
   - For unknown messages, we need to assign senders such that:
     - The sender is different from the previous message's sender
     - The sender does not appear as a word in the message text
     - The sender is one of the available users

3. Since we need any valid assignment, we can use a greedy approach:
   - Process messages sequentially
   - For each unknown message, try all possible senders that satisfy the constraints
   - If no valid sender exists, output "Impossible"

4. To check if a username appears as a word in text:
   - Split text into words (using space and punctuation as delimiters)
   - Check if the username appears as a complete word

## Step-by-step Implementation

```python
def get_words(text):
    """Extract words from text (sequences of letters and digits)"""
    words = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(text):
        if text[i].isalnum():
            word = ""
            while i < len(text) and text[i].isalnum():
                word += text[i]
                i += 1
            words.append(word)
        else:
            i += 1
    return words

def does_mention(text, username):
    """Check if username appears as a word in text"""
    words = get_words(text)
    return username in words

def solve_chat():
    n = int(input())
    usernames = input().split()
    m = int(input())
    
    messages = []
    for _ in range(m):
        line = input().strip()
        if line.startswith('?'):
            messages.append(('unknown', line[2:]))
        else:
            parts = line.split(':', 1)
            messages.append((parts[0], parts[1]))
    
    # Create a mapping from message index to sender
    sender_assignment = [-1] * m  # -1 means unknown
    
    # Fill in known senders
    for i in range(m):
        if messages[i][0] != 'unknown':
            sender_assignment[i] = messages[i][0]
    
    # Process unknown messages
    for i in range(m):
        if sender_assignment[i] == -1:  # Unknown message
            # Find valid sender for this message
            valid_senders = []
            
            # Check all users
            for user in usernames:
                # Skip if this sender would be same as previous message
                if i > 0 and sender_assignment[i-1] == user:
                    continue
                
                # Skip if sender mentions themselves
                if does_mention(messages[i][1], user):
                    continue
                
                # Add valid sender
                valid_senders.append(user)
            
            # If no valid sender found, impossible
            if not valid_senders:
                return "Impossible"
            
            # Choose any valid sender (greedy choice)
            sender_assignment[i] = valid_senders[0]
    
    # Validate the final assignment - check consecutive messages
    for i in range(1, m):
        if sender_assignment[i] == sender_assignment[i-1]:
            return "Impossible"
    
    # Build output
    result = []
    for i in range(m):
        if sender_assignment[i] == -1:
            result.append(f"{messages[i][1]}")  # This shouldn't happen due to logic
        else:
            result.append(f"{sender_assignment[i]}:{messages[i][1]}")
    
    # Verify that all known senders are properly handled
    for i in range(m):
        if messages[i][0] != 'unknown' and sender_assignment[i] != messages[i][0]:
            return "Impossible"
    
    return "\n".join(result)

# Main execution
t = int(input())
for _ in range(t):
    result = solve_chat()
    if result == "Impossible":
        print("Impossible")
    else:
        print(result)
```

Model Response

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have t chats, each with n users and m messages
2. Some messages have unknown senders (marked with "?")
3. Constraints:
   - No two consecutive messages can have the same sender
   - A sender cannot mention themselves in their message
   - If a user mentions themselves in text, that message cannot be sent by them
4. We need to assign senders to unknown messages or output "Impossible" if no valid assignment exists

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Parse the input and extract information
- Read the number of chats
- For each chat: read users, messages, and process each message

### Step 2: For each message with unknown sender (?)
- Check which users can send it (users who don't mention themselves in the text)
- Also ensure it doesn't conflict with adjacent messages (different from previous and next)

### Step 3: Use backtracking or greedy assignment
- Since constraints are small (n ≤ 100, m ≤ 100), we can try a systematic assignment
- For each "?" message, check all valid options that satisfy:
  1. The user doesn't mention themselves in the text
  2. The user is different from the previous message's sender
  3. The user is different from the next message's sender (if it exists)

### Step 4: Handle edge cases
- If no valid assignment exists for any "?" message, output "Impossible"

Let me implement this:

```python
def get_mentions(text, usernames):
    """Extract all usernames mentioned in the text"""
    mentions = set()
    words = []
    # Extract words by splitting on non-alphanumeric characters
    i = 0
    while i < len(text):
        if text[i].isalnum():
            word_start = i
            while i < len(text) and text[i].isalnum():
                i += 1
            word = text[word_start:i]
            words.append(word)
        else:
            i += 1
    
    # Check if each word is a valid username
    for word in words:
        if word in usernames:
            mentions.add(word)
    
    return mentions

def can_send_message(message_text, sender, usernames):
    """Check if a sender can send a message (doesn't mention themselves)"""
    mentions = get_mentions(message_text, usernames)
    return sender not in mentions

def solve_chat(users, messages):
    n_users = len(users)
    m_messages = len(messages)
    
    # Create a list to store the final message assignments
    result = []
    
    # First, identify which messages have known senders and which have unknown
    known_senders = []
    unknown_positions = []
    
    for i, msg in enumerate(messages):
        if msg.startswith('?'):
            unknown_positions.append(i)
        else:
            # Parse known message
            parts = msg.split(':', 1)
            sender = parts[0]
            text = parts[1]
            result.append((sender, text))
            known_senders.append((sender, text))
        else:
            # This is a known message
            parts = msg.split(':', 1)
            sender = parts[0]
            text = parts[1]
            result.append((sender, text))
            known_senders.append((sender, text))
    
    # Initialize result with known senders
    final_result = []
    for i, msg in enumerate(messages):
        if msg.startswith('?'):
            final_result.append((None, msg))
        else:
            parts = msg.split(':', 1)
            sender = parts[0]
            text = parts[1]
            final_result.append((sender, text))
    
    # Fill in unknown senders
    # We'll use a greedy approach: for each unknown message, try to find a valid sender
    # that doesn't mention themselves and doesn't conflict with neighbors
    
    # First, check if we have any conflicts with known senders
    for i in range(m_messages):
        if final_result[i][0] is not None:
            continue
            
        # Find all valid senders for this unknown message
        valid_senders = []
        for user in users:
            if not can_send_message(final_result[i][1], user, users):
                continue
            # Check if it conflicts with previous message
            if i > 0 and final_result[i-1][0] is not None and final_result[i-1][0] == user:
                continue
            # Check if it conflicts with next message
            if i < m_messages - 1 and final_result[i+1][0] is not None and final_result[i+1][0] == user:
                continue
            valid_senders.append(user)
        
        if not valid_senders:
            return "Impossible"
        
        # Choose one valid sender (any is fine)
        chosen_sender = valid_senders[0]
        final_result[i] = (chosen_sender, final_result[i][1])
    
    # After assignment, verify that no two consecutive messages have the same sender
    for i in range(m_messages - 1):
        if final_result[i][0] == final_result[i+1][0]:
            return "Impossible"
    
    # Verify that no sender mentions themselves
    for sender, text in final_result:
        if sender is not None:
            if not can_send_message(text, sender, users):
                return "Impossible"
    
    # Format the output
    output_lines = []
    for sender, text in final_result:
        output_lines.append(f"{sender}:{text}")
    
    return "\n".join(output_lines)

def main():
    t = int(input())
    
    for _ in range(t):
        n = int(input())
        users = input().split()
        m = int(input())
        messages = []
        for _ in range(m):
            messages.append(input().strip())
        
        result = solve_chat(users, messages)
        if result == "Impossible":
            print("Impossible")
        else:
            print(result)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

```python
def get_mentions(text, usernames):
    """Extract all usernames mentioned in the text"""
    mentions = set()
    words = []
    # Extract words by splitting on non-alphanumeric characters
    i = 0
    while i < len(text):
        if text[i].isalnum():
            word_start = i
            while i < len(text) and text[i].isalnum():
                i += 1
            word = text[word_start:i]
            words.append(word)
        else:
            i += 1
    
    # Check if each word is a valid username
    for word in words:
        if word in usernames:
            mentions.add(word)
    
    return mentions

def solve_chat(users, messages):
    m_messages = len(messages)
    
    # Create result array with None for unknown senders

    for msg in messages:
        if msg.startswith('?'):

            text = parts[1]
            result.append((sender, text))

        if result[i][0] is not None:
            continue
            
        # Find valid senders for this message
        valid_senders = []
        for user in users:
            # Check if user mentions themselves
            if user not in get_mentions(result[i][1], users):
                # Check constraints with neighbors
                prev_sender = result[i-1][0] if i > 0 else None
                next_sender = result[i+1][0] if i < m_messages - 1 else None

                if prev_sender is not None and prev_sender == user:
                    continue
                
                # Cannot be same as next message
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Recently Vladik discovered a new entertainment — coding bots for social networks. He would like to use machine learning in his bots so now he want to prepare some learning data for them.

At first, he need to download t chats. Vladik coded a script which should have downloaded the chats, however, something went wrong. In particular, some of the messages have no information of their sender. It is known that if a person sends several messages in a row, they all are merged into a single message. It means that there could not be two or more messages in a row with the same sender. Moreover, a sender never mention himself in his messages.

Vladik wants to recover senders of all the messages so that each two neighboring messages will have different senders and no sender will mention himself in his messages.

He has no idea of how to do this, and asks you for help. Help Vladik to recover senders in each of the chats!


-----Input-----

The first line contains single integer t (1 ≤ t ≤ 10) — the number of chats. The t chats follow. Each chat is given in the following format.

The first line of each chat description contains single integer n (1 ≤ n ≤ 100) — the number of users in the chat.

The next line contains n space-separated distinct usernames. Each username consists of lowercase and uppercase English letters and digits. The usernames can't start with a digit. Two usernames are different even if they differ only with letters' case. The length of username is positive and doesn't exceed 10 characters.

The next line contains single integer m (1 ≤ m ≤ 100) — the number of messages in the chat. The next m line contain the messages in the following formats, one per line:   <username>:<text> — the format of a message with known sender. The username should appear in the list of usernames of the chat.  <?>:<text> — the format of a message with unknown sender. 

The text of a message can consist of lowercase and uppercase English letter, digits, characters '.' (dot), ',' (comma), '!' (exclamation mark), '?' (question mark) and ' ' (space). The text doesn't contain trailing spaces. The length of the text is positive and doesn't exceed 100 characters.

We say that a text mention a user if his username appears in the text as a word. In other words, the username appears in a such a position that the two characters before and after its appearance either do not exist or are not English letters or digits. For example, the text "Vasya, masha13 and Kate!" can mention users "Vasya", "masha13", "and" and "Kate", but not "masha".

It is guaranteed that in each chat no known sender mention himself in his messages and there are no two neighboring messages with the same known sender.


-----Output-----

Print the information about the t chats in the following format:

If it is not possible to recover senders, print single line "Impossible" for this chat. Otherwise print m messages in the following format:

<username>:<text>

If there are multiple answers, print any of them.


-----Examples-----

1
2
Vladik netman
2
?: Hello, Vladik!
?: Hi

Output
netman: Hello, Vladik!
Vladik: Hi

Input
1
2
netman vladik
3
netman:how are you?
?:wrong message
vladik:im fine

Output
Impossible
